# HiGO-R2 vs GO-GPT, BioReason-Pro and the compare_paper baselines: evaluation suite on GO-GPT's temporal test (extended)

Generated by `python -m cafa6.eval_suite --extended` (`src/cafa6/eval_suite.py`; metric definitions, statistics and scoring protocol in its module docstring, identical to `runs/eval_suite/report.md`). The default run and its outputs in `runs/eval_suite/` are unchanged; this directory adds the methods scored in `runs/compare_paper/` as rows and as comparisons. Every prediction file was frozen before this run; nothing was tuned, selected or changed on these numbers, and no test look is recorded. All numbers below come from the tsv files next to this report.

Ground truth: `data/proc_gogpt/gt_test.tsv`, 8,630 proteins (MF 2080, BP 5819, CC 3440 with annotations); ontology `runs/gogpt_cafa5/ref/go-basic_gogpt.obo`, IA `runs/gogpt_cafa5/ref/IA.txt`, cafaeval protocol (prop=max, norm=cafa, tau 0.01..0.99), scores in the 3-decimal submission format. Brackets are paired protein bootstrap 95% CIs (1000 reps, seed 0, the same draws for every row). "mean" is the mean of the three aspects.

Rows:

- **HiGO-R2** `runs/stacker_tv/l3_c0/pred_test_twin.npz`; **GO-GPT ten-sample** `runs/gogpt_sample10/gogpt_sample10_freq.tsv.gz`; **GO-GPT beam** `runs/gogpt_cafa5/preds_shard*.jsonl`; the two `[component]` rows (inputs of HiGO-R2, not models; no tests): as in `runs/eval_suite`.
- **GO-GPT greedy** `runs/gogpt_cafa5/greedy/preds_shard*.jsonl` (released checkpoint, greedy decoding, our generation; sets with score 1).
- **BioReason-Pro RL** `runs/compare_paper/preds/bioreason_rl.npz`: released `wanglab/bioreason-pro-rl`, the authors' `eval.py` with the published-benchmark settings of `scripts/sh_eval.sh` (greedy, ask_all_go_aspects=False, force_uniprot_summary=False); GO ids are taken from the whole response with score 1, as `evals/cafa_evals.py` does. In `cafa6/compare_paper.py` this is method key `bioreason_rl`, the row of `runs/compare_paper/summary.tsv` with S3 IA-weighted Fmax mean 0.6490. `bioreason_rl_final.npz` (final-answer block only, 0.6478) and the label-independent prompt run (0.6471) are variants and are not rows here.
- **BLAST-KNN (training refs)** `preds/blast_knn.npz` (paper section 4.4.2: best DIAMOND hit by percent identity among GO-GPT training proteins, all its propagated terms with score identity / 100); **BLAST-KNN (train+val refs)** `preds/blast_knn_trainval.npz` (same rule, training + validation proteins as references, the reference set of HiGO-R2's kNN); **DIAMONDScore** `preds/diamondscore.npz` (bitscore-weighted label mean over the top 50 hits); **Naive** `preds/naive.npz` (per-aspect training term frequency, the same vector for every protein).

The seven compare_paper rows (and, for the cross-check, nothing else) are read with `cafa6.compare_paper.load_method`, the loader behind `runs/compare_paper/summary.tsv`. Not included as rows: the GO-GPT authors' `go_pred` file, the two BioReason-Pro variants, and the BioReason-Pro SFT catalogue (1,213 test proteins only).

## 0. Cross-checks

Against the official cafaeval output (`runs/test_look/look3_l3c0_twin/cafaeval/best_*.tsv`, the three original rows, as in `runs/eval_suite`): max |diff| of Fmax / Fmax_w 2.0e-04 in the submission format and 2e-15 for the HiGO-R2 4-decimal replica (the HiGO-R2 file given to cafaeval was rounded to 4 decimals; see the v1 report).

Against `runs/compare_paper/summary.tsv` (S3_all, written with 4 decimals by `knn_sharp.Scorer`, the in-memory cafaeval copy): IA-weighted Fmax, IA-weighted S-min and unweighted Fmax for MF, BP, CC and the mean, for every row that has a compare_paper key. **Check passed: max |diff| Fmax_w 4.9e-05, Smin_w 4.9e-05 (required < 1e-3), Fmax 5.0e-05; Fmax_w thresholds equal in 45 of 45 aspect rows.** Differences of this size are the 4-decimal rounding of summary.tsv. In `crosscheck.tsv` these rows have `scores` = "vs runs/compare_paper/summary.tsv S3_all (knn_sharp.Scorer, 4 decimals)" and the compare_paper value in the `cafaeval` column.

The BioReason-Pro row (0.6490) scores at the level of the GO-GPT authors' predictions that its prompt contains (0.6494 in `runs/compare_paper/summary.tsv`) and below the released GO-GPT checkpoint runs here. BLAST-KNN abstains on proteins without a DIAMOND hit, so its coverage stays below the hit rate (table above) and its precision is averaged over fewer proteins. What it does not show: Fmax is one operating point per aspect, chosen on the test set for every row (the CAFA protocol), and precision averages only over covered proteins.

**Flag: GO-GPT beam, GO-GPT greedy, BioReason-Pro RL.** These rows have binary scores (one distinct value in the raw file), so their micro curve has two operating points (score 1 and score 0) and their protein curve one; their AUPR values are close to precision x recall at that point and measure one operating point, not a ranking. The AUPR tests against them carry the flag in `pvalues.tsv`. Not flagged but close to it: BLAST-KNN (training refs), BLAST-KNN (train+val refs) give every term of a protein the same score (BLAST-KNN: the identity of the best hit), so within a protein there is no ranking; the curve points come only from differences between proteins. Naive gives the same vector to every protein, so it ranks terms but not proteins.

What it shows: mean IA-weighted micro AUPR by row: HiGO-R2 0.7093; InterLabelGO+ (leaked) 0.6914; [component] kNN prior (train+val refs) 0.5970; BLAST-KNN (train+val refs) 0.5426; GO-GPT ten-sample 0.5288; BLAST-KNN (training refs) 0.5284; DIAMONDScore 0.4845; SPROF-GO 0.4613; GO-GPT beam 0.4468; GO-GPT greedy 0.4399; [component] HiGO head alone 0.4030; BioReason-Pro RL 0.3952; DeepGOPlus 0.2604; ProteInfer (5-model ensemble) 0.2028; DeepGO-SE 0.1869; Naive 0.1229; DeepFRI (sequence CNN) 0.1032. Mean IA-weighted protein-centric AUPR: HiGO-R2 0.7376; InterLabelGO+ (leaked) 0.7112; [component] kNN prior (train+val refs) 0.6467; BLAST-KNN (train+val refs) 0.5853; BLAST-KNN (training refs) 0.5694; GO-GPT ten-sample 0.5432; DIAMONDScore 0.5413; SPROF-GO 0.5212; GO-GPT beam 0.4752; GO-GPT greedy 0.4749; [component] HiGO head alone 0.4497; BioReason-Pro RL 0.4287; DeepGOPlus 0.3419; ProteInfer (5-model ensemble) 0.2721; DeepGO-SE 0.2258; DeepFRI (sequence CNN) 0.1638; Naive 0.1580. HiGO-R2 minus each row on AUPR_micro_w: vs GO-GPT ten-sample +0.1806 [+0.1706, +0.1906] (CI above 0 in MF, BP, CC); vs GO-GPT beam +0.2626 [+0.2527, +0.2729] (CI above 0 in MF, BP, CC); vs GO-GPT greedy +0.2694 [+0.2593, +0.2795] (CI above 0 in MF, BP, CC); vs BioReason-Pro RL +0.3141 [+0.3022, +0.3250] (CI above 0 in MF, BP, CC); vs BLAST-KNN (training refs) +0.1809 [+0.1700, +0.1922] (CI above 0 in MF, BP, CC); vs BLAST-KNN (train+val refs) +0.1668 [+0.1561, +0.1780] (CI above 0 in MF, BP, CC); vs DIAMONDScore +0.2249 [+0.2147, +0.2356] (CI above 0 in MF, BP, CC); vs Naive +0.5864 [+0.5745, +0.5980] (CI above 0 in MF, BP, CC); vs SPROF-GO +0.2481 [+0.2356, +0.2595] (CI above 0 in MF, BP, CC); vs DeepGOPlus +0.4489 [+0.4368, +0.4604] (CI above 0 in MF, BP, CC); vs ProteInfer (5-model ensemble) +0.5065 [+0.4950, +0.5172] (CI above 0 in MF, BP, CC); vs DeepGO-SE +0.5224 [+0.5102, +0.5338] (CI above 0 in MF, BP, CC); vs DeepFRI (sequence CNN) +0.6062 [+0.5940, +0.6179] (CI above 0 in MF, BP, CC). As in the v1 report, AUPR mixes ranking quality with score resolution and with how many pairs a row scores at all (recall ceiling); rows with coarse or binary scores are at a structural disadvantage that says nothing about their top predictions.

F1_w on proteins without a DIAMOND hit to a training protein: InterLabelGO+ (leaked) 0.327; SPROF-GO 0.319; GO-GPT beam 0.311; GO-GPT greedy 0.306; GO-GPT ten-sample 0.286; BioReason-Pro RL 0.267; [component] HiGO head alone 0.231; DeepGO-SE 0.189; ProteInfer (5-model ensemble) 0.179; Naive 0.167; HiGO-R2 0.133; DeepFRI (sequence CNN) 0.129; DeepGOPlus 0.050; [component] kNN prior (train+val refs) 0.027; BLAST-KNN (train+val refs) 0.022; BLAST-KNN (training refs) 0.000; DIAMONDScore 0.000. The homology rows (BLAST-KNN, DIAMONDScore, the kNN prior) have nothing to transfer there (the train+val BLAST-KNN and kNN prior can still hit a val protein), while the generative rows and Naive predict for every protein. What it does not show: the low-identity bins are small (see n), so their CIs are wide, and per-protein F1 at a global threshold is not Fmax.

What it shows: mean Spearman(F1_w, train identity) by row: BLAST-KNN (training refs) 0.600; BLAST-KNN (train+val refs) 0.570; [component] kNN prior (train+val refs) 0.528; HiGO-R2 0.523; GO-GPT greedy 0.431; GO-GPT ten-sample 0.431; GO-GPT beam 0.430; BioReason-Pro RL 0.401; DIAMONDScore 0.394; InterLabelGO+ (leaked) 0.300; [component] HiGO head alone 0.191; ProteInfer (5-model ensemble) 0.116; DeepGOPlus 0.110; SPROF-GO 0.095; DeepFRI (sequence CNN) 0.076; Naive 0.051; DeepGO-SE 0.017. A larger value means per-protein quality tracks homology more closely; Naive has no protein-specific input, so its correlation comes only from which proteins are easy (shallow, frequent annotations). What it does not show: the correlation is dominated by the large high-identity bins, and a higher correlation together with a higher mean is not a weakness by itself.

## 7. GO-relation retrieval from learned term vectors

HiGO and GO-GPT rows are those of `runs/eval_suite` (same vectors, vocabularies and targets). Targets come from 76201 direct is_a/part_of edges of go-basic_gogpt.obo; cosine similarity, all other vocabulary terms ranked; mean over terms with >= 1 target.

BioReason-Pro has term vectors inside its GO graph encoder, so it is added: the authors' `GOGraphEncoderUnified` (`external/BioReason-Pro-github/bioreason2/models/go_graph_encoder.py`) was built on CPU with the RL checkpoint's `go_encoder.pt` and the precomputed term embeddings (`/dev/shm/cmp_paper_go_emb/go_embeddings`, 43248 terms, 2560-d). It runs 3 GAT layers over the obonet graph of the authors' go-basic.obo (84805 edges, all relation types: is_a 69350, part_of 6851, regulates 3157, negatively_regulates 2729, positively_regulates 2718), then a 2-layer projection back to 2560-d, then a cross-attention reducer to 200 vectors; only those 200 vectors reach the LLM, so per-term vectors exist only inside the encoder. Recomputing the 200 reduced vectors from the loaded weights reproduces the checkpoint's cached `go_embedding.pt` to relative L2 error 3.1e-03 (min row cosine 1.0000), so the encoder is loaded as the model uses it. Rows: the per-term encoder output (input of the reducer), the last GAT layer (512-d), the precomputed input embeddings (frozen, not trained by BioReason-Pro), and the same encoder at fresh initialisation on the same inputs (5 seeds; what the graph propagation gives without training). 43248 terms map to a unique term of go-basic_gogpt.obo; on the common vocabulary of HiGO and GO-GPT BioReason-Pro has 7898 of 7898 terms. Run time 2.3 min on CPU.

Untrained rows are means over 5 seeds; the largest seed sd of any P@k / R@k is 0.0054 (`retrieval.tsv`).

What it shows (common vocabulary, direct neighbours, P@1 / R@10): HiGO effective query 0.678 / 0.724; HiGO construction untrained 0.384 / 0.401; GO-GPT wte 0.154 / 0.126; BioReason-Pro encoder output 0.668 / 0.771; BioReason-Pro last GAT layer 0.667 / 0.782; BioReason-Pro input embedding 0.677 / 0.724; BioReason-Pro encoder untrained 0.666 / 0.782; random 0.000 / 0.001. On its own 43248-term vocabulary BioReason-Pro's encoder output has direct P@1 0.675 against 0.668 for the untrained encoder (seed sd 0.005) and 0.513 for its input embeddings. So almost all of its neighbourhood structure is there before training: the precomputed inputs already place GO neighbours close, and three rounds of message passing over the GO graph (is_a, part_of and the regulates relations) average each term with its neighbours whatever the weights are. The same holds, less strongly, for HiGO's ancestor-mean construction (untrained row above). What it does not show: none of these models is trained for GO-neighbour retrieval, BioReason-Pro's LLM never sees these per-term vectors (only the 200 reduced ones), and the numbers say nothing about prediction quality.

## 8. Multiple testing

Paired permutation tests (10000 reps, seed 1; two-sided; smallest attainable p 1.0e-04), Holm-Bonferroni (`p_holm`) and Benjamini-Hochberg (`q_bh`) within each family, `p_holm_all` / `q_bh_all` over all 650 tests.

Primary family (364 tests): {Fmax, Fmax_w, Smin_w, AUPR_micro, AUPR_micro_w, AUPR_prot, AUPR_prot_w} x {MF, BP, CC, mean} x {HiGO-R2 vs each of the 13 comparison rows: GO-GPT ten-sample, GO-GPT beam, GO-GPT greedy, BioReason-Pro RL, BLAST-KNN (training refs), BLAST-KNN (train+val refs), DIAMONDScore, Naive, SPROF-GO, DeepGOPlus, ProteInfer (5-model ensemble), DeepGO-SE, DeepFRI (sequence CNN)}.

Secondary family (286 tests): mean-over-aspects F1 and F1_w in the 7 train-identity bins (14) and the Spearman(F1 or F1_w, train identity) difference for MF, BP, CC and mean (8), x 13 comparison rows.

What it shows: 355 of 364 primary tests have Holm p < 0.05 (356 with BH q < 0.05; 0 with Holm over all 650 tests). Significant primary differences in favour of the comparison row: Fmax_w BP vs BLAST-KNN (train+val refs) (-0.0107). Not significant after Holm (9): Fmax MF vs GO-GPT ten-sample (-0.0026, Holm p 1.000), Fmax CC vs GO-GPT ten-sample (+0.0009, Holm p 1.000), Fmax_w CC vs GO-GPT ten-sample (+0.0122, Holm p 0.101), Fmax mean vs GO-GPT ten-sample (+0.0037, Holm p 0.787), Fmax MF vs GO-GPT beam (+0.0011, Holm p 1.000), Fmax MF vs GO-GPT greedy (+0.0001, Holm p 1.000), Fmax_w MF vs BLAST-KNN (training refs) (+0.0104, Holm p 0.595), Fmax_w MF vs BLAST-KNN (train+val refs) (+0.0051, Holm p 1.000), Smin_w BP vs BLAST-KNN (train+val refs) (-0.1717, Holm p 1.000). Secondary family: 227 of 286 pass Holm, 246 pass BH. What it does not show: the tests condition on the 8,630 test proteins; a p at the minimum only says the observed difference exceeds every swapped one; metrics within a family are strongly correlated, so Holm is conservative.

## Files

Same names and columns as `runs/eval_suite/`: `headline.tsv`, `auprc.tsv`, `smin.tsv`, `identity_bins.tsv`, `spearman.tsv`, `consistency.tsv`, `retrieval.tsv`, `differences.tsv` (bootstrap CIs of HiGO-R2 minus each comparison row), `pvalues.tsv`, `crosscheck.tsv` (cafaeval rows as in v1, plus the compare_paper rows), `summary.tsv`; `log.txt` is the console output of the run.
